The distinction: SILENT (a) is where the theory explicitly takes something as an input (declared inputs: boundaries, obligations, normative relations, contract scope). SPLIT is where the theory's own sentences are genuinely two-sided (ambiguous or supporting both constructions). For O1: is Bruno's rule's status an "input"? Not a declared input of Part XIV. The theory's sentences about tables support two verdicts. So SPLIT fits my blind reading. OK, going with SPLIT for O1.

Ugh. OK let me re-read the SPLIT and SILENT definitions VERY carefully:

"SPLIT: on at least one point, the theory's sentences support two readings that reach different findings, and the text leaves the choice between them open."
"SILENT: on at least one point, the theory leaves the finding open, because it takes the deciding matter as an input the case leaves unstated, or because its sentences stop short of deciding it."

Key difference: SPLIT = the theory's sentences support two readings (of the point) with different findings; the choice is open in the text. SILENT = the theory leaves the finding open (no verdict), because (a) the deciding matter is an input the case doesn't state (e.g., declared boundary, obligations, worth), or (b) the sentences stop short.
